---
name: portal-builder
description: >
  Recipe for building ANY new application or portal the way AstroStage and tv.valuestream.in are
  built: every decision is a chip made of generic ops, owner-only admin screens (User Access, User
  Access Control, Portal Activity, Memory Watch, Visitor Dashboard, How to Use config, Setup +
  SMTP), individual screens (Core Business, Key Management), a fixed sign-up / sign-in / verify /
  forgot / reset flow (AUTH.md), and one information flow whatever the host (Cloudflare, Vercel,
  Render, VPS). Use when starting a new portal or site, porting a module into one, adding a screen
  to tv/ or another core/-based portal, or reviewing one against the standard.
---

# Portal Builder

Every portal we build has the same skeleton, whatever its business is. AstroStage (six brand
domains, one engine) proved the architecture. `tv/` (tv.valuestream.in) made it portable: a
zero-build, standard-library framework in `tv/core/` that you copy. This skill says what every new
portal must have, in what order to build it, and which traps have already cost us time.

Read first, in this order:
1. `docs/AstroStage_Architecture.html` — zones, request journey, "every reading is data", delivery,
   standby, rings of defence.
2. `tv/core/README.md` — the framework modules, env vars, chips & ops grammar.
3. `AUTH.md` (beside this file) — the sign-up and sign-in standard. **It is not optional.**
4. `.agents/skills/ops-chips-first/SKILL.md`, `.agents/skills/ui-popups/SKILL.md`,
   `.agents/skills/paid-calls/SKILL.md`, `.agents/skills/guide-me-script/SKILL.md` (every screen's Guide me).

---

## 1. The prime directive: chips and ops, from the first line

> "Every thing should be built at the lowest level: Chips and Ops." (owner, 2026-09-17)

| Layer | Holds | Never holds |
|---|---|---|
| **Op** (`core/ops.py`, AstroStage `backend/engine/*_ops.py`) | A generic, pure primitive: `median`, `pct`, `filter_rows`, `group_rows`, `rank_rows`, `decision_table`, `window_live`, `template`… Unanswerable input → `None`. | The domain. An op never knows what a memory limit, a book or a visitor is. |
| **Chip** (`core/chips/*.json`, `<portal>/chips/*.json`, AstroStage `engine/data/chips`) | ONE named decision: `params` = knobs with defaults, `expr` = ops + other chips, `test_cases`, `audience: admin` for platform chips. File name = `chip_id`. | I/O. A chip is a pure function of its arguments. |
| **Rule** (`core/rules/*.json`, `<portal>/rules/*.json`, AstroStage FYI rules) | WHAT THE PORTAL DOES: at a trigger (`server.sample`, `signup.submit`, `upload.start`, `page.view`, `daily`…), IF a test over the trigger subject's facts holds, THEN outcomes (`refuse`, `banner`, `notify`, `value`, `flag`). Knobs, named values, test cases on scenarios. | Code. Python only registers subjects/triggers/outcome kinds and calls `rules.fire(trigger, facts)`. |
| **Python module / router** | Gathers FACTS (read `/proc`, query rows, call SMTP, check a session) and asks chips: `engine.run("memory.verdict", facts)`. Enforces who may call it. | A threshold, a message, a ranking, a policy, a TTL, a regex that decides something. |
| **Page (JS/TSX)** | Draws what the server returns; UI state (tab, page, open/closed). | Re-validation of what the server decides. Ask the server (`dry_run`). |

Grammar (identical in AstroStage and `tv/core/engine.py`):

```json
{"chip": "memory.spike", "args": {"events": "$events"}}
{"vop": "median", "values": "$rss_series"}
{"vop": "choose", "cases": [{"when": {"op": "ge", "left": "$pct", "right": "$warn_pct"}, "then": "warn"}], "else": "ok"}
{"op": "all|any|not|eq|ne|in_set|ge|gt|le|lt|at_least", ...}
"$knob_or_argument"
```

Tests are tri-state: True / False / None (unknown). **Unknown never passes.**

Every converted module ships three guards, each bite-tested (break the code once, see the check
fail, restore):
1. Its chips certify: `python -m core.engine [portal/chips]` (refs resolve, every test holds).
2. A ratchet that the module still calls each of its chips and defines no inline judgement
   (example: `tv/selfcheck.py` memory block — `engine.run("memory.verdict"` must appear, `def verdict` must not).
3. A behaviour check of the facts side (example: `core/memwatch.py` against a fake `/proc` tree).

**Astrology vs portal — same engine, different inputs.** An AstroStage chip reads a CHART (golden
charts are its test data); a portal chip reads FACTS (scenarios are its test data). An FYI rule turns
into an outcome card for a reader; a portal rule turns into refuse / banner / email / value / flag.

Every chip and rule is visible and editable in the two builders (the two-way rule): **Ops & chips**
(OCB — ops catalog, chips with knobs, expression tree, tests, usages, history, scenarios, op switches)
and **Rules** (RCB — trigger → knobs → named values → IF → THEN → dry run on a scenario → tests).
Life: draft → review → publish (refused while any problem or failing test remains); return with a
note; switch off (refused while used); restore any version as a draft. Builder writes live under
`data/builder/` (never git). Roles `owner > editor > author > member`, decided by chip `builder.can`:
author drafts/submits own work, editor publishes, owner also deletes and switches ops off; framework
chips (`core/` or `audience: admin`) are the owner's. Sign-ups are members and never see the builders.

---

## 2. One information flow, any host

```
visitor ─▶ EDGE (Cloudflare / Vercel / none) ─▶ web server (nginx) ─▶ app middleware ─▶ router ─▶ chips ─▶ store
                                                  only door open      auth + rate limit      facts     decisions   JSON / SQLite / Postgres
                                                                      + activity ledger
```

Hosting varies; the flow, storage shape and presentation do not.

| Choice | AstroStage | tv.valuestream.in | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Frontend | Cloudflare Pages (6 domains, one build) | Served by the app (zero build) | Either. Hostname picks shell/title/palette when one build serves many brands. |
| API | `api.astrostage.in`, CF-proxied → VPS Mumbai | Subdomain → VPS directly | ONE stable API name so failover never needs a rebuild. |
| App process | systemd uvicorn `127.0.0.1:8001` | systemd `tv-portal` `127.0.0.1:8810` | Loopback only. Only nginx faces the network. Check the port is free first — a clash answers with the OTHER service's 400/404. |
| Data | PostgreSQL 18 in docker (loopback) | `data/*.json` + `data/ledger.sqlite3` | Small state JSON; append-heavy logs SQLite (stdlib); relational multi-user data Postgres. |
| TLS | CF Origin CA, Full (strict) | certbot --nginx | Never plain HTTP to the origin. |
| Client IP | `CF-Connecting-IP` | `X-Real-IP` / last XFF hop | `PORTAL_TRUSTED_PROXY=cloudflare|nginx|none`. First XFF entry is attacker-chosen. |
| Visitor place | `cf-ipcountry` / `cf-region` | local DB-IP mmdb (`core/geo.py`) | CDN header wins, else local DB; nothing sent out. |
| Backups | hourly `pg_dump`, 7 days | hourly tar of `data/` | Backups are separate from any standby. |
| Standby | Render, 15-min one-way copy, manual failover | none | Never mix tiers across regions (200 ms/query). |
| Secrets | `.env` root-only | systemd `Environment=` | Never in git, never echoed (not even in a diagnostic script). |

Delivery: push to `main` is the only release trigger. The owner commits and pushes — make edits
only, never `git commit`/`push`. After every change say what restarts: `.py` → service restart;
static JS/CSS → hard refresh (tv serves `/static` and `/core-static` with `no-cache`);
AstroStage `.tsx` → rebuild.

### Research-institute workspaces (2026-09-27, AstroGaami first)

Full recipe: `.agents/skills/institute-onboarding/SKILL.md`.

Another institute that wants the ecosystem gets a WORKSPACE, never a fork. The institute owns
content; the platform owns code.

- **Tenant = the existing `Institute` row.** `User.institute_id`, `UserRole(institute_id)`,
  `UserGroup` per institute and `owner_institute_id` already exist. Add no new tenant table.
- **Config = one file** `backend/data/workspaces/<slug>.json`: brand (name, palette), hosts,
  `entry` (`signed_in` | `members`), `visibility` (`members` | `public` — only whether its rules show
  in AstroStage's shared catalogs), and `screens[]` (registry control keys + tab functions).
- **Door = chip `access.workspace_door`** (`{enter, join}`): with `entry: signed_in` every AstroStage
  account enters on the workspace's own **Catch All · <Name>** group, can self-join ("Become a member"),
  and its Workspace Admin assigns groups (institute-onboarding §6a). One door for the page, its rules
  (`inactive_for`), the site switcher and `/join`.
- **Workspace Admin** (`UserRole workspace_admin` + `institute_id`) runs the portal's own admin screens
  scoped to the workspace (`services/admin_scope`, header `X-Workspace`; screen list = chip
  `access.workspace_admin_screens`). All access policy is chips (institute-onboarding §6e).
- **Host:** `<slug>.astrostage.in` or `?ws=<slug>`, read by `src/services/workspace.ts`.
  `App.tsx` routes it to `WorkspacePortal`, which is one shell for every institute.
  Tracking uses surface `workspace` and module "Institute workspaces". How-to key: `<slug>-workspace`.
- **Their doctrine = their rules.** Use rule id prefix `<code>_*` (`ag_*`) with
  `metadata.workspace: <slug>` and `surfaces: [<slug>]`.
  - `inactive_for` 404s a rule to whoever the door keeps out.
  - `listed_hidden` keeps it out of the shared catalogs.
  - Every constant of theirs (scores, thresholds, keyword lists, hour mode) goes in the rule's
    JSON, as a knob on a GENERIC op (`tara_matrix`, `hora mode`). Never an `if institute ==` branch.
    When their logic differs from ours, add a knob with their value as the default. Never "fix" it
    silently.
- **Zero typed input:** chart = My Charts subject; stars/lords/panchang/sunrise = engine ops.
- Onboard institute #2 with: workspace JSON + rules + tour JSON + a How-to key. No new code.

### Tenant wall (HARD, every portal with tenants — owner's rule 2026-09-28)

No tenant ever sees another tenant's data: AstroGaami never sees AstroGravity; one person who
belongs to several tenants shows, inside each, only that tenant's slice. The same law as the
per-account wall (`owner_id`, 404), one level up:

- **Every admin/report route is tenant-aware on the server.** AstroStage: `Depends(admin_scope("<view>"))`
  + filter on `scope.ws` — never plain `require_portal_admin`, which ignores the tenant, so a
  Portal Admin standing inside a workspace would see the whole portal. tv/core portals with more
  than one tenant: the same shape (a scope object from the request, filters on it).
- **Returns only the tenant's own:** its members, its roles/groups/home institute on a person, its
  screens, its activity and visits, its help pages, its own tenant row.
- **Portal-wide data returns nothing inside a tenant** (every tenant list, the portal's own access
  requests, users' portal assets/subscriptions, portal permissions, edge/CDN traffic of every
  domain, other sites' activity). **Portal-wide actions are refused** (delete user, set password,
  approve requests, create tenant, archive, bulk copy) — `admin_scope.portal_only`.
- **UI mirrors, server enforces** (hide portal panels in a tenant; the route must refuse on its own).
- Guard with one person in TWO tenants, data in both, called from inside one — the other must never
  appear, refusals asserted as 403 (AstroStage: `test_workspace_admin.py::test_no_institute_sees_another*`).
- Full table: institute-onboarding §6d; module rules: module-builder §7a.

## 3. The screens every portal has

### Owner-only admin (the portal OWNER sees these; nobody else, whatever a rule says)

Exception, by design: a tenant's own admin (AstroStage **Workspace Admin**) sees the listed
screens SCOPED to their tenant — never the portal-wide versions (tenant wall, §2).

Figures ABOUT other people (storage per person, usage, who keeps what) are owner-only too: `portal_admin`, never "admin or granted" -
a moderator grant is for handling reports, not for looking at people (ValueConnect Storage by person, owner 2026-10-29). A store or
catalog sits at the top level for everyone; an in-context menu (a chat) lists only what the person owns.

| Screen | tv/core | AstroStage reference | Must do |
|---|---|---|---|
| **User Access** | `access.py` People tab, `page-access.js` | Portal Admin → User Access | List people, roles, invite/add, **Reset Password** (AUTH.md §6), remove. |
| **User Access Control** | `access.py` rules tab (Groups & rules), chip `access.decide` | Portal Admin → Access Control (`authz.py`) | The AstroStage model (2026-10-19, every portal): built-in **Catch All** (renamable, never deleted) = what an account in no group sees; a group member sees only what their groups (and the groups they **inherit**) allow, plus personal rules; **deny wins**; owner never locked out; "Check a person" with the reason; access requests; a **flexi search** over the screens (list `access_screens`, op `fuzzy_rows`). Day one = migration where nobody loses a menu. |
| **Portal Activity Ledger** | `ledger.py`, `page-activity.js` | Portal Activity + Reports | One body-free row per request (names and ids only — never a body, query string, token or message). Reports by person/screen, archive download then clear. |
| **Memory Watch** | `memwatch.py` + `memory.*` chips | `MemoryWatch.tsx` | Verdict in words, whole server (CPU/memory/disk/bandwidth: portal vs other), offenders, recent moves. |
| **Reported issues + Push to** | `core/issues.py`, `core/push.py`, `page-issues.js`, chip `issues.push` | Portal Admin → Issue Reports → Push to (`services/issue_push.py`, chip `ui.issue_push`) | Resolve with a reply; **Push to** (owner only, open issues) sends the issue to CodyFy as a request through its MCP door - every FY portal into company **ValueStream** (project = the portal name), AstroStage into **AstroGravity** - then the row follows its stage with a link. Key pasted once, checked before kept, owner-only file. domain-portal skill "Issue > Push to CodyFy". |
| **Visitor Dashboard** | `ledger.py` + `geo.py`, `page-visitors.js` | `VisitorDashboard.tsx` | Views, visits, pages, devices, referrers, dwell; **world map + India map by state** (`core/static/geo/`). |
| **How to Use Page Configuration** | `howto.py`, `page-howto-config.js` | `HowToUseConfig.tsx` | Master/detail, searchable grouped list with a count badge per row; links show on every page's How to Use button. |
| **Setup** (+ **SMTP email**) | Setup + `mailer.py` | Key Management / settings | Portal name and address, sign-up mode, SMTP with a Test button and Gmail App-Password help. |
| Storage (when users hold files) | `storage.py` | — | Per-account usage; clean-up request ×3 (3 days apart), delete from day 7, enforced server-side. |

### Build (owner, editor, author — never member)

| Screen | tv/core | AstroStage reference | Must do |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ops & chips (OCB)** | `builder.py`, `page-ocb.js`, `builder-ui.js` | `OpsChipBuilder.tsx` | Ops / Chips / Scenarios tabs; chip knobs, logic tree, tests, check, usages, history; op switch-off (owner); macro chip from an op. |
| **Rules (RCB)** | `rules.py`, `builder.py`, `page-rcb.js` | `ChipRuleBuilder.tsx` | Trigger → knobs → named values → IF → THEN (typed outcome fields) → dry run on a scenario → tests → publish. |

Server enforces owner-only: `decide()` answers no for any `admin` screen before reading rules;
someone else's id → **404, not 403** (a 403 confirms the row exists). A selfcheck ratchet fails any
`/api` route with no screen/owner/signed-in guard.

### Individual level (every signed-in person)

| Screen | What |
|---|---|
| **Core Business** | The portal's reason to exist (tv: Video Narrator, Manual Builder). Its decisions are the portal's own chips in `<portal>/chips/`. Every row carries `owner_id`; listings are per person. |
| **Key Management** | Each person's OWN provider keys (no portal-wide key, no env fallback — one person must never spend another's balance *unseen*). Empty secret box + Show + **Paste** button + live length; **Save and test** (a real 4-word call on that key, booked like a paid run); never judge a key by prefix or length; strip control characters on save. Provider funds: loaded amount − ledger spend, warn below a level. Every paid call shows provider, model, cost, time, progress and money left (`paid-calls` skill). **Lending a key** — see below. |

#### Lending a key (2026-09-23, `tv/keyshare.py` + `keyshare.*` chips)

A key may be **lent** to another account on the same portal, and taken back. The rules, all of them
in chips:

1. **Own first, then borrowed.** `providers.pool()` = `own_keys()` + `keyshare.borrowed()`. Nobody
   spends someone else's balance while their own key works.
2. **One seam, not fifteen call sites.** `providers.key_for` / `call_with_keys` stamp the key into
   `store.IN_USE` (a `ContextVar`, per thread, matched on *who asked* + *which provider* so a reused
   thread cannot bill the wrong account). `store.spend` books the **key owner's** balance and
   `store.log_run` stamps `key_id` / `key_owner_id` / `shared` on the run. A new paid feature needs
   no sharing code at all.
3. **A borrowed key is not yours.** It cannot become your default (that would copy the secret into
   your own key box) and you cannot delete it — you *give it back*. Renaming one IS yours: the
   borrower's label (usually the lender's name) and the owner's label are separate fields.
4. **A share is never deleted.** It keeps `granted_at`, `revoked_at` and who ended it
   (`keyshare.grant_state` → active / revoked / returned).
5. **The lender sees the money, not the work.** `keyshare.name_seen` gives the key's owner only the
   first N characters of what the borrower called a run; the borrower sees it whole and can check a
   bill against it. Same rule for the error text and token counts.
6. **Bills are frozen.** `push_bill` sums the runs since the last bill and stores the totals, so the
   bill still reads true after the receipt roll (`store.settings.runs_kept`) has rolled past them.
   Raise `runs_kept` before lengthening the gap between bills.
7. **Usage across the board.** The usage table covers own keys too, not only lent ones.
8. **The lender's terms (mark-up + caps).** A share carries `markup_pct` and a rolling cap per day /
   week / month. `keyshare.price` turns a run's real cost into what the borrower is charged; the
   per cent is **frozen onto the run row** at `store.log_run`, so raising it later never re-prices
   work already done. The lender's *balance* still moves by the real cost — a mark-up is a debt
   between two people, never a second charge from the provider. The borrower sees the charged price
   and nothing else (`base_usd` is stripped from the bill they receive); the lender sees both.
9. **A cap stops the key, it does not warn.** `keyshare.limit_block` measures the CHARGED spend in
   each rolling window and sets `blocked` on the borrowed key; `providers.key_usable` refuses a
   blocked key, so it drops out of `candidates()` and the existing "no usable key" path explains it.
   Rolling hours, not calendar months — no time zone, no month length.
10. **Quote before you promise.** `keyshare.rate_card` tells a borrower roughly what a minute of
    video, a still and a thousand words will cost *them* (mark-up in, in their currency) and always
    carries the disclaimer saying what moves that number. The lender sees the same quote live while
    typing the mark-up. Never show the estimate without the disclaimer.
11. **Sitting.** `core/store.note_session` (called from `auth.current_user`) keeps a digest of the
    session token per person in memory, so a background job's run can be grouped per sitting in the
    lender's report. The token itself is never written to a row.

#### Selling credit on a key (`tv/keysales.py` + `keysales.*` chips)

A **sale is a lending that was paid for up front**, so it reuses every rule above rather than forking
them. `decide_order(approve=True)` calls `keyshare.share` with the offer's mark-up, **caps 0** and
`cap_total_usd` = the amount bought, then `keyshare.raise_bill(kind="purchase")`.

- **Credit is the `total` window.** `keyshare.limits` has a fourth window whose `total_hours` is 0,
  meaning "since the share began" — it never refills, and `owing()` skips prepaid grants so nobody is
  billed twice for the same runs.
- **Who may sell is a chip list**, not a hardcoded role: `keysales.may_sell.seller_roles`. Adding a
  Sales Person = a row in `people.roles` + its id in that list. Nothing in Python changes.
- **Billing only, never payment.** Approving raises a bill; the two people settle it outside and mark
  it paid. `keysales.settings.billing_only` says so on every screen. Do not add a payment step here
  without the owner asking.

##### A screen that does not exist yet (generic, reusable)

"Buy a key" must be **absent**, not locked, until somebody sells one. Three pieces, all in the
framework so any portal can use them:

1. `core.access.register_available("<screen>", fn)` — `fn(user) -> bool`, "is there anything here for
   this person at all?"
2. chip `access.decide` gains fact `available`; a `no` refuses **before every other rule, the owner
   included**.
3. the catalog entry carries `hide_when_denied: true`, and `shell.js` drops such a screen from the
   menu instead of showing it greyed.

Use this for any screen that is meaningless until somebody configures it. A screen that is merely
*not permitted* stays visible and locked — that is a door to knock on, and the difference matters.

Plus the way in: **Sign up / Sign in / Verify / Forgot / Reset** — see `AUTH.md`.

---

Never-again 2026-10-27 (Cloudflare Pages): one file in `public/` over **25 MiB** fails the WHOLE Pages deploy
  (`voiceid/campplus.onnx` 27.9 MiB blocked 3 prod deploys, site stuck on the old build). Ship big assets in parts
  joined in the browser (`connect/fetchParts.ts`, chip `voice_id.model_parts`) or serve them from the backend.
  Guard `src/publicAssetSize.test.ts`.

Never-again 2026-10-28 (vc.valuestream.in shared on WhatsApp showed the AstroStage card): a host that serves the SAME
build as another portal shows THAT portal's title, og:/twitter: tags and icons to link-preview robots (they run no
script, so a runtime `document.title` never reaches them). Every such host swaps its head tags where it is served:
Cloudflare Pages -> `functions/_middleware.js` HOSTS; nginx -> `sub_filter` in its site block (live copy in
`deploy/vc-site/`), with its own 1200x630 `public/<brand>-og.png`. Guard `connectApp.test.tsx` (a shared vc link).

Never-again 2026-10-28 (ValueConnect, 20 calls x 10 people): a page that polls must poll for what is ON SCREEN.
  Per person the old call screen asked ring + messages(200) + call + list + unread every 3-8 s (1.2 req/s; 200
  people saturated one uvicorn, p50 7.5 s). Now: the still-here beat carries the ring answer, a covered chat does not
  poll, a chat beside a call polls every `poll_in_call_ms` for the newest `poll_page`, last_seen writes at most every
  online_seconds/4, a YES access check is kept 15 s (a NO never), and a page outside Studio runs no Studio nav polls
  (`NO_NAV`). Busy routes get their own process (`ASTROSTAGE_JOBS=0` = no server jobs; `deploy/connect-helper/`).
  Guards `src/components/connect/callPlugin.test.tsx` ('a call asks the server little'), `backend/tests/test_connect.py`,
  `test_function_guards.py`, `test_db_pool_no_deadlock.py`. Windows-only: Memory Watch's process sweep is n x n - throttled.

## 4. Build order for a new portal

1. **Copy the framework.** `tv/core/` into the new repo (it never imports host code). Add
   `catalog.json` (your screens), `app.py` (`core.install(app, catalog="catalog.json", chips="chips")`),
   `static/app.js` (`startPortal({views, pitch, …})`), `chips/`. Run `python selfcheck.py` style
   checks from day one.
2. **Auth to the AUTH.md standard** before any business screen. Reserve the owner seat
   (`PORTAL_OWNER_EMAIL`); first visitor with that address becomes owner.
3. **Admin screens** come with core; confirm each is owner-only in the selfcheck.
4. **Core Business**: write the ops you lack (generic!), then chips, then routes that gather facts
   and call chips, then the page. Every screen gets a `catalog.json` row, a view, `Depends(screen(...))`.
5. **Key Management** if anything costs money.
6. **Deploy** (section 2). Every screen ships its live **Guide me** tour in the same change (skill
   `guide-me-script`; on tv/sm the chip `guide.<screen>`, `screen-tour-guide`) — the ratchet only shrinks.
   The kit (`core/static/guide-kit.js` + `.css` + `icons.js`) is portable: a new portal copies it with `core/`.
   **Every CHANGE to a screen too (owner, HARD, 2026-10-02: "if we update/create a module/screen we need to update
   Guide me and the Tour Video")**: a new or changed module / screen / feature updates, in the SAME change,
   (a) its Guide me chip `guide.<screen>` (the live button) AND (b) its Tour Video script `tours/sm-<screen>.json`
   (Emma's narrated recording). Then open **Tour Video** (sm, Document group, owner only): the row turns
   "Script changed since the video" (chip `tourvideo.state`) - Check steps, then Record video. Guards: every sm
   screen has both; a page drawing `finalCheck(` / `activityCard(` / Feedback must be taught by both.
7. **Share package** if the portal is handed out: copy minus `data/`, `.venv/`, `__pycache__`; scrub
   the owner's email; run selfcheck inside it; rebuild the zip. Keep it in step with EVERY change.
8. **Agent + MCP scope check (owner, HARD, 2026-10-23)** — every portal, every screen, every new
   feature: run the test in `module-builder` §8a (agent = repeat / schedule / many records / draft →
   approve; MCP tool = read-only fact-in-fact-out op, useful outside, no other person's data). Answer in
   the end-of-turn line **Agent / MCP**. Propose, build after the owner says yes; agents are recipe
   chips on the one runtime, MCP tools are rows in chip `mcp.tools` — never per-agent or per-tool code.

---

## 5. The UI bar

### ONE standard: learn one screen, know them all (owner, HARD, 2026-09-28)

Owner: "if you know how to use MS Word then you exactly know how to use MS Excel, MS PowerPoint". Every portal, every
address of a portal (a door such as katha.valuestream.in), every screen looks and behaves the same:
1. **Code quality / modularity** - one module per job; a second portal or door REUSES the shell (`startPortal`), it never
   gets a page of its own with its own header, sign-in or styles.
2. **No duplicate code for the same function** - one sign-in card (core `shell.js`), one file box (`dropZone` +
   `wireDrops`: browse, drop, paste), one dialog (`modal`), one "are you sure" (`confirmDialog`), one Report
   (`reportIssue` -> core Reported issues), one list (`listBox`, server-paged), one page header (`pageHead`), one empty
   state (`emptyState`), one busy button (`busy`). Two screens drawing the same thing share ONE module (Katha: the
   Read stories screen and the public `/k` page both draw through `static/katha-view.js`).
3. **UI standard** - every delete warns in an App Message; Browse/Upload, OK, Cancel, Delete, Add, Edit, Sign in look
   and sit the same everywhere; wording comes from chips.
4. **Sentence case** (owner, 2026-10-02) - every button, field label, column head, tab, popup title, status and
   job-window line reads "Import a deck", never "Import A Deck". Capitals stay only on NAMES: screen / module /
   product names ("Doodly Videos", "Publish & Circles", "Key Management"), people, places, brands, jyotisha terms.
   A screen name keeps its caps where it names that screen; a label that merely contains it does not ("Generate
   Chart" menu item, "Generate chart data" button). No Tailwind `capitalize` (it Title-Cases every word) - use
   `first-letter:uppercase` (+ `inline-block` on a span). Guard `backend/tests/test_sentence_case.py` scans chip
   words + tsx/tv JS literals; a real name it flags goes in `backend/data/ui_case_names.json`, never a code exception.
Before shipping a screen or a door: grep it for `/api/auth/signin`, `<input type="file"`, `window.confirm`, its own
`<style>` block or header - each is a second standard. Guards (tv `selfcheck.py` api part): one sign-in form (only
`shell.js`), no bare file input outside `dropZone` (backlog `page-manual.js`, `page-studio.js`, shrink-only), `/` on
every host is the one shell.

  `view === '…'` in App.tsx; EXEMPT = whole brand apps; BACKLOG only shrinks).

  first header on the screen, never twice. NEVER a button row above the header, never a floating help pill. Guard

  top"):** inside a shell, ANY `<HowToUse>` / `<HowToBuild>` written deeper in a screen is lifted into the header
  card (`common/pageHelpContext` `useHelpHoist`, portal into the card's slot) - write it where the section is, it
  shows at the top. While a section shows its own Guide me / How to Use, the shell's general one steps aside: never
  two "Guide me". Stays put: help inside a popup, a compact "?" on a tile, `guide={false}` links. Workspace uses the
  same provider (no "?" in its top bar); its institute modules draw `PageHeader flush compact`, never an `<h2>` title.
  Guard `src/components/common/pageHelpHoist.test.tsx`.

  `<PageHelpProvider pageKey="…" inherit>`: standalone it provides the key, inside a shell the shell's provider wins.
  The individual portal's `SectionShell` IS the standard card now (it renders `PageHeader`; `compact` for the book
  shelf) - one header look in the whole portal; a shelf's own How to Use goes in its `actions`. Another shell's own
  header card can still take the buttons with `usePageHelpSlot()`. `PageHelpFallback` = a row ONLY on a screen that has no header card.
- **One layout on every reading screen (HARD, 2026-09-30 owner: "a place for everything and everything in its place" -
  the zoom bar sat above the header on one Run Time screen and below it on the next).** Order, top to bottom: reading

- **One play area per parent menu (HARD, 2026-09-30 owner: "Run Time is used by more people and they need more play
  area; Portal Admin and Utilities are managed by a few - consistent within the parent menu").** Every Studio-shell
  screen sits in `common/PageFrame` (`group` from `menuGroupOf(view)`; the Run Time menu list wins over admin
  ownership). Width + padding per menu group = chip `ui.page_frame` (`frames`: `standard` 1500px / `wide` = whole
  window; `group_frames`: runtime → wide). Never write a `<main>` width or a per-screen max-width wrapper. Guards
  `src/components/common/pageFrame.test.tsx`, `backend/tests/test_ui_chips.py`.
- **One popup size per parent menu (HARD, same day, same chip).** `ui.page_frame` `popups` (standard 1152px × 85vh, large
  1600px × 92vh, own = untouched; height = cap, taller scrolls inside the panel) + `group_popups` (runtime/studio → large, the rest → standard) + `popup_small_up_to`
  (px, default 576: a popup BUILT this wide or narrower - confirmations, short prompts, default Modals - keeps its size;
  `PopupSizeMark` tags it `data-popup-small` from its own `max-w-*` class, arbitrary `[Npx]` included; 0 = resize all). `PopupSizeMark` (mounted once in App; Utilities popup open → group
  `utilities`) writes `data-popup-size`, `data-popup-w`/`-h` + `--popup-max-w`/`-h` on `<html>`; ONE block at the end of `src/index.css` resizes
  every popup panel (direct child of `fixed inset-0` carrying a `max-w-*`), common/Modal included. Never size a popup
  for its menu in the popup; build it `w-full max-w-*` and the rule does the rest.
  Reach: direct child of the overlay, or one down inside the `min-h-full` centring wrapper. HEIGHT is capped only
  where nothing can be clipped: a panel that is not `overflow-hidden` (it then scrolls inside itself), or an
  `overflow-hidden` one that sets its own height (has a scrolling body, e.g. Modal). An `overflow-hidden` panel with
  NO height relies on the overlay scrolling - never cap it (content would be cut off out of reach). Guard
  `pageFrame.test.tsx`: every overlay in src is reached or listed (pages / spinners), list only shrinks.
- **Top-menu panel widths = chip too** (`ui.page_frame` `menu_panels`, px per menu group). Mega-menus, `MenuPanel`
  (`group` prop - every one in App names it) and the Bookmarked panel read `useMenuPanelStyle(group)`; the `w-[…]`
  class left on a panel is only the pre-chip fallback. Never add a panel width in code.
- **Other portal shells = chip too** (`ui.page_frame` `shells`: `workspace`, `individual`, `study`, `familyos`, `gravitas`, `unwind`, `tedbook`, `grahauvacha`, `astrosahai`, `astrounwind`, `public_viewer`, `auth` (sign-in / sign-up screens) -
  page_max_width, sidebar, sidebar_collapsed, own menus / pages). The shell root spreads `useShellVars('<shell>')` into its style (`--shell-*`), and every
  width class reads one: `max-w-[var(--shell-page-max-width,1600px)]` (after the comma = pre-chip fallback). Variables,
  not inline widths, so `lg:` rules still decide when a width applies. A new shell adds a `shells` entry. Guard
  `pageFrame.test.tsx` (every chip width used, no width read that the chip lacks).
  Each shell also mounts `<PopupSizeMark group="workspace|individual" />` (popup size per shell = `group_popups`), and
  the profile menu shared by all portals takes `menu_panels.profile`.
  A shell that HOSTS shared screens (workspace, individual) sets `content_max_width` (0 = the whole content column):
  `useShellVars` publishes it as `--frame-max-width`, the variable every shared screen reads first - same as the Studio frame.
  Marks NEST (Gravitas / UnWind run standalone and inside the Studio shell): the innermost - last in the document -
  decides, and the outer one is restored when it closes. Never clear the `<html>` flags by hand.
  An app that also runs inside the Studio frame caps its page with `max-w-[var(--frame-max-width,var(--shell-page-max-width,…))]`:
  `PageFrame` publishes `--frame-max-width`, so the frame governs when framed and the shell width only standalone (Gravitas, Study, UnWind, GrahaUvacha, TeDBook shelf).
- **Master/detail** over table+modal for browse-and-edit lists; count badges; grouped + searchable.
- Popups: portalled, Escape closes, pinned close bar and pinned FOOTER, panel scrolls inside (`ui-popups`).
- Refresh button top-right on every page, redraws in place, asks before discarding typed edits.
  Theme and menu-fold buttons never re-render the page. No duplicate page-level Refresh.
- Container queries for panes (a narrow pane is narrow whatever the window). Measure layout; don't eyeball.
- Mobile: burger menu, nothing horizontal-scrolls, a landing page that fits one screen at 1366×630.
- No chart/map library needed: SVG drawn from data (`page-memory.js`, `geo-map.js`); dark mode via
  `color-mix` on brand tokens.

---

### Common structure for content that grows (tv / sm / katha, 2026-10-01 owner: "assess ... and create a common structure which can support all. Chips and ops only")

One codebase (`tv/`) serves tv.valuestream.in, sm.valuestream.in and katha.valuestream.in by edition. Every module
(Comics, Doodly, Avatar, Films, Audiobooks, TeDBooks, Decks, Assessments, Katha) uses the SAME parts - never its own:

1. **Reading data** (`core/store.py`): `read` = your own copy (you will change and write it); `view(name)` = the ONE shared
   parsed copy, READ-ONLY, re-parsed only when the file changes (`write` drops it at once); `index(name, key)` = rows by
   key from that copy; `row(name, id)` = one row as your own copy (indexed); `user_by_id`; sessions by token. A per-row,
   per-card, per-cover or per-request path never parses a whole file. Tripwire: `store.views_intact()` in each Katha
   self-check (a reader that changes the shared copy is caught).
2. **A collection on screen** = `lists.source(id)` + chip `list.<id>` (search keys, sorts, page_size <= 10-25) + `listBox`
   (or the one `assetHome`). The server pages first; costly per-row work goes in `fill` (the page only). Never `.map(`
   a whole collection into page HTML, never a `<select>` / tab bar over a collection that grows.
3. **What this server has** (libraries, fonts, model files, ffmpeg answers): looked up once per chip `check_every_s`
   (`avatar._kept`), a failed ffmpeg still kept per file version (`media._NO_STILL`); one-time costly starts warmed at
   boot in a background thread (`avatar.warm`), never in a reader's list.
4. **Abuse** = ONE gate `core/limits.allow(action, who, n)`, numbers in chip `limits.rates` (per_s, most, why); zips =
   `limits.zip_room(zf)` (chip `limits.files`); uploads = `app.upload_bytes(u, file, cap + 1)` (cap + the person's quota).
5. **Who may see**: the check lives where the rows are made (e.g. `katha._may_open_place` inside `_shelf_rows`), so the
   generic `/api/lists/<id>` road cannot skip it.

Ratchets (`tv/selfcheck.py` asset_checks / katha_groups_check): `narrator.listing` always with `user=`; no raw upload read
outside `upload_bytes`; module `get()` = `store.row`; `works()` / `circles()` / `groups()` = `store.view`; no per-open
stats rewrite; Groups / Publish & Circles lists are listBox with page_size <= 10.

**pickList** (`core/static/ui.js`, 2026-10-01 Phase 3): ONE search box over the first page of `/api/lists/<id>` (says how
many more; Enter takes the first; Esc closes) - the way to CHOOSE from a growing collection; `listBox` is the way to
SHOW one. Done: Publish "what to publish" (list.katha_publishable, readiness in `fill`), Katha shelf places
(list.katha_places), Following authors (list.katha_following, one pass over works), deck Add a work
(list.decks_pickable), Buy a key offers (list.buy_offers), Combine bin (list.video_usable), comic cast + picture
character (list.studio_characters). Ratchet: tv/selfcheck.py asset_checks (old pattern gone, `list: '<id>'` used, chip exists).

Phase 3 part 2 done: YouTube + TeDBook Homes send ONE record (`?id=`) not all; image library film filter + Use for a
shot (list.image_films); comic place suggestions one page as you type (list.studio_places; the comic sends only places
its panels use); Manuals (list.manuals, listBox); How to Use Add a recording (list.help_recordings, pickList).

Phase 3 part 3 done: YouTube attach (list.my_recordings, pickList); Studio series cards = newest few (chip studio.series
shelves_shown) + "N more"; admin Storage detail = the biggest few (chip list.storage_people items_shown) + count, Delete
all still every item. Left on purpose: Studio reuse-from (lectures only; showing the panel needs the full check),
projects select (a person's own projects, few by nature), Katha lists (capped 50 by chip), comment replies, access
rules (admin), banners - each small by design; revisit if one grows.

Phase 4 done (2026-10-01): every DELETE asks first (+ Studio draft removes); popup footers and 6 rows wrap; empty states
<= 24px padding; the tab title / boot line name the site (`app.page(host)`, edition door); 9 buttons no longer greyed on
first open (empty click says what to do; words ui.shell need_file / need_text ...); greyed-by-state counted per file,
only goes down; security headers for every answer from chip `security.headers` (nosniff, Referrer-Policy,
frame-ancestors, X-Frame-Options, HSTS on https) in core `_stamp_build`; edition guard `app.edition_routes` + chip
`edition.route_screens` (a SteadyMove-only address answers 404 on tv). All in tv/selfcheck.py (asset_checks + api),
each bitten.

**Left after Phase 4:** ~85 buttons greyed BY STATE (no key, nothing picked, list ends) - ratcheted, convert to the
`aria-disabled + data-off + title` pattern (ui.js aiPick) screen by screen; a full CSP (script/style) - inline styles
everywhere; nginx `server_tokens off` (server, not repo); owner decisions: key lend / wallet by unverified email,
copyright claim auto-hide. Was: ~47 buttons greyed on first open; 3 deletes without asking
(doodle recording, Studio reference x, Studio shot); `.modal > footer` and ~7 rows without wrap; tall empty states;
index.html title; site guards (routes of screens an edition lacks) + security headers (no CSP / X-Frame-Options /
nosniff / Referrer-Policy anywhere; katha has no HSTS - nginx, not in the repo); key lend / wallet by unverified email
(owner decision); copyright-claim auto-hide policy (chip katha.settings claims_hide).

### 5x. UEW on every wait (owner 2026-10-11)
Every portal shows the User Engagement Window for any wait of ~3 s or more - activities, elapsed, time left,
total, Continue in background. React = `common/ProgressDialog`; a non-React portal ports the same window into its
shared UI kit. Full rule: skill `ui-popups` "UEW".

### 5y. Get ready - never a bare "not set up" (owner 2026-10-14: "this should be a common component and this is how it should be everywhere")
Any action that cannot run until something is set up (a key, a machine, a sign-in, a grant, a service) shows the
common step-by-step guide instead of an error line or a greyed button: done steps ticked (with who / which), the first
missing step marked Now with what to do and its ONE button, each step ticking itself from server facts, and the work
starting by itself (or one press) when all are ticked. React = `common/GetReady` (+ `useOpenOnce`,
`useStartedAfterReady`); server = `services/get_ready.checklist(keys, facts)`; the steps + order live in the feature's
chip, the words in its word chip (`<prefix>.title/.lede/.now/.step.<key>/.do.<key>/.all_set`). First user: the Claude
popup (chip ui.infrastructure get_ready). Guard `src/components/common/getReady.test.tsx` (no hand-rolled step list).
**AI key / permission** = `common/AiReady job=<job>` on GetReady: chip `ui.ai_ready` (jobs chip_chat, llm_plus,
narration, transcript: steps, providers, key source, grant), route `GET /user-api-keys/ai-ready`; Add a key opens Key
Management in place, an admin also gets Access Control; when ticked the caller carries on (`onReady`). A route that
refuses for want of a key answers `{message, key_missing: true, provider}` (rule_engine `_key_missing`), never bare text.
Guards `backend/tests/test_ai_ready.py`, `src/components/common/aiReady.test.tsx` (every listed door uses it).
**An outside service refuses the SERVER** (YouTube "Sign in to confirm you're not a bot" on the VPS, 2026-10-17): say it
in plain words (a word chip, e.g. `ui.yt_blocked`), fold the tool's raw text behind Details, route the call to a
company machine on a home / office connection when one reports it can (`services/machine_jobs`, chip-declared job,
spec docs/youtube_via_machine.md), and always offer a manual way in on the same screen (Paste the transcript, chip
`yt.paste_lines`). Never a Google account's cookies on the server, never advice to add them. Guard
`backend/tests/test_yt_via_machine.py`.

## 6. Security and robustness (before any public launch, and re-run before it)

- Server-side authz on every route; owner-only admin; per-account walls (`owner_id`, 404);
  **tenant wall** — no tenant sees another's data, portal-wide data empty and portal actions refused
  inside a tenant (§2 "Tenant wall").
- Rate limits: auth calls per IP per minute; lockout after N wrong passwords; per-address mail
  throttle; public beacons limited and write-only. Numbers are chips (`auth.*`), not constants.
- No account enumeration: forgot/request-code answers identically for a stranger and a real account.
- Tokens/codes single-use, hashed or random, short TTL; never in logs, ledger or query strings kept.
- Trusted proxy set correctly (section 2); nginx `location` blocks each set `X-Real-IP`.
- Uploads: cap at the door AND mid-stream; `client_max_body_size` in step with the app cap.
- Scans/paths: a non-owner never reads server paths (`PORTAL_SCAN_ROOTS`).
- Gate nothing on an unconfigured dependency: no SMTP → degrade to the useful path (AUTH.md §7),
  never to a refusal. Opening a door = building its walls in the same change.
- Routes that don't `await` are plain `def` (threadpool); a sync body in `async def` freezes the worker.
- Content growth: store counters (`bytes`), don't walk the disk per request; memory watch catches
  load-everything endpoints.

---

## 7. Every fix, forever

For each defect: fix at the shared layer (core/, not one page), add a guard, **prove it bites**,
record the lesson (this skill's registry below or the governing skill), save a memory. Tests never
write into the repo. Never start the user's backend — verify in-process or with a static harness,
and stop anything you started.

A shape thicker than `fill_px` is drawn as its rim + the edges on it (`edge_min`), the inside comes with the colour. crf 18 / medium. Picture prompt bans glow/blur/ghosting refs. ## Never-again: menu shown without access (2026-10-03)
A managed user must never see a menu trigger with no granted screen under it, nor land on a blocked
screen when another is granted. Items: `MenuButton view=`; triggers: `IfAnyScreen` (`src/lib/access.tsx`);
landing: `LandingFallback` (Studio via `StudioLanding`). Guard `src/lib/access.test.tsx`.
tv / SteadyMove shell: `screens()` = `may()` only; shut screens asked for via user menu `#ask-more`. Guard `tv/selfcheck.py` asset_checks.

## Never-again: a page-load badge that starves sign-in (2026-10-10)

A badge or counter fetched on EVERY page load must never start heavy work per request. Check Sync's drift
badge (`rules-sync/audit`) ran a ~40 s cold audit per call; after a backend reload each load (and StrictMode's
double fetch) started its own, timed-out requests kept their threads, and the pile starved `/auth/me` past the
browser's 10 s timeout - TeDBook and AstroStage showed SIGNED OUT, Tour Video renders failed. Rule: heavy work
behind a per-load endpoint is single flight (`rules_sync_check.audit`: one runs, callers that arrive meanwhile
share it and get their own row copies), plus a cache for the warm path. Guard
`backend/tests/test_rules_sync_single_flight.py` (bites: 4 audits without the gate). Same shape as
`engine/book_rows.py` `_BUILDING` and `chip_chat._BOOK_GATES`.

## Indian rules first (HARD, owner 2026-10-10)

"We are operating in India mostly and we need to follow Indian rule. Period." Where a choice differs by country, the
Indian rule wins. Maps: India's official boundary only - all of Jammu & Kashmir (incl. PoK, Gilgit-Baltistan) and
Ladakh (incl. Aksai Chin) are India; no LoC, no dashed "disputed" edge, no UN view. Our sources already comply: world =
Natural Earth 'IND' POV (`src/components/admin/geo/worldCountries.ts`), states = DataMeet official
(`indiaStatesRobinson.ts`); any third-party map picture is repainted to it before it ships

- **2026-10-19 Every list comes with a flexi search (owner: "Any list should come with flexi search").** People & access -> Groups & rules showed 34 screens with no way to find one. Rule: every list, table, picker or grid an owner or user scrolls has a forgiving search box - a typo, a half word or a sub-item's name (a function under its screen) still finds the row, nonsense finds nothing, identifiers (email, user name, code, number) stay exact - a forgiving search over 5,000 look-alike emails returned 1,769 for one; the box says how many match ("2 of 34"), offers a clear button, and an empty result shows a picture plus a next step, never a bare blank. The SEARCH is the server's: a `core/lists.py` list whose `list.<id>` chip sets `fuzzy_min` (op `fuzzy_rows`); a grid already on the page asks that list for the matching ids and hides the rest. One look everywhere: `.fx-find` / `.fx-none` in `core/static/portal.css` (words in the page's ui chip). First: `list.access_screens` + `page-access.js` `#rule-find`; guard: `core/access.py` self-check (`"sound frm"` finds Sound from Video, a function name finds its screen, nonsense finds nothing, non-owner 403) - bitten by forcing `fuzzy_min` 0.
- **2026-10-19 One access model in every portal (owner: "apply AstroStage logic to all the portal").** tv/sm/Katha and every -Fy had an Everyone group with "nothing said = open" and a per-screen "Only allowed" switch, while AstroStage used Catch All + managed groups + inherit + deny wins - the same person could see different menus for the same rules. Rule: one model, AstroStage's, in `core/access.py` + chip `access.decide` (knobs `access.settings`: `new_group_inherits_catch_all`, `new_screens_to_catch_all`, `personal_rule_manages`); a model change ships with a day-one migration proven on copies of every live user list (0 menus changed) and the old resolver kept only as the proof's yardstick. Guard: `python -m core.access` (migration, inherit, deny wins, lent admin screen) in every portal.
- **2026-10-26 Live is not open (AstroFy, owner: "Astrocartography visible to everyone").** A Menu builder draft is an admin screen until Live, so `new_screens_to_catch_all` saw it as NEW on going live and added a Catch All allow - the owner's "Super User only" screen opened to all. Rule: `access.data()` remembers EVERY catalog id (drafts too) in `seen_screens` as it arrives; Live opens to nobody new, Access Control alone decides. Guard: `python -m core.access` ("a draft put live opens to nobody until allowed").
- **2026-10-26 Framework CSS on a host's own page (AstroFy, owner: names unreadable).** A host page that imports `core/static/portal.css` for the bottom bar gets its BARE element rules too (label = 11px bold UPPERCASE grey, b = `--ink`, which the host may use for its background). Rule: the host's embed sheet hands every such element back outside the widget roots (`:where(tags):where(:not(:is(roots) *)){all:revert}`, unlayered, zero specificity). Every portal also needs its own `valuestream.site.json` (mail sender + mark, CC listing) - a copied smtp.json sent AstroFy's codes as "Justify". Guard: AstroFy `test_framework_elements_never_restyle_astrofy`, `test_mail_goes_out_as_astrofy`.
- **2026-10-19 Look at every changed screen before saying done (owner: "Did you really check all the UI").** CodyFy's Projects form got Folder on the machine + Machine side by side in a two-column grid; all checks passed and I reported done, but the folder box sat lower than the machine box, a different height, its example cut off - and the same grid misaligned Build line / Folder it makes. Rule: before a UI change is reported, screenshot every changed form, card and dialog in every state (empty, filled, each select option that shows or hides fields), light and dark - on the live page, or a harness that uses the real page code, the real CSS and the live modal frame - and say which were looked at. Forms put one field per row (side by side only for twin fields with no help text); a placeholder is a short example, advice goes in the help line under it.

## Never-again: a feature key missing from saved copies (2026-10-20 CodyFy)

A new key added to a chip's `events` broke the "standard = the default" check and would have been missing from every company's
saved workflow. Derive new behaviour from keys that already exist, or migrate the saved copies in the same change. Also:
a Try it / preview never refuses a start line it can still run - the machine reads the port the app prints (inbox
`_ports_said`) instead of asking a non-technical person to fix a command.

## Never-again: a coach that points at another screen's parts (2026-10-22 CodyFy Set up)

Set up's coach (`static/coach.js`, steps in chip `getstarted.settings`) walks a new person through the REAL Code Editor by
pointing at its ids (#rq-new, #rq-send, [data-go=promote] ...). Renaming one of those on the Code Editor would leave the person
stranded with no error anywhere. Rule: any guide that points into another screen keeps its targets as chip data AND a guard reads
that screen's source and fails when a target is no longer drawn (`selfcheck_getstarted.static_check`, bitten by renaming
rq-new on a copy). Also: a step's own claim (the request the coach saw being written) must be the writer's - the company owner
sees every request, so "can see it" is not "wrote it" (bitten on a copy by dropping the author check). A coach panel never
covers what it points at (right, left, then top), names the button by its live label ({label}), and follows a dialog that the
step opens (`then`).

Also (same day): a tour script REBUILT from its Guide me (CodyFy `tools/make_tours.py`) silently dropped clicks added to it by hand
(cf-requests' click that opens the New request form), so the next recording showed the wrong screen. A rebuild now keeps the actions
of every step whose chapter + words are unchanged, and `selfcheck.guides_tours_check` fails when a rebuild would change any tour
(bitten with the old builder on a copy).

Also (same day, owner: "I can't believe commit script breaking"): a guard that shelled out to `bash` passed in Git Bash but broke
the owner's Commit-To-GitHub, which runs from PowerShell where `bash` is Windows' WSL stub (exit 1, no words). A guard that calls
an outside program proves it runs first (`exit 0`), prefers a known install (Git for Windows' bash beside git.exe), and skips that
part saying so when none works. The last check before handing over is the full selfcheck run exactly as the commit script runs it.

Also (REQ-0002, the first real Mac): the Mac setup line ran `curl https://claude.ai/install.sh | bash </dev/null` - the redirect
REPLACES the pipe, so bash ran an empty script; Claude Code never installed while the next line printed "Found: Claude Code".
Rule for every setup script: never redirect the stdin of a shell that reads a pipe, and every "Found" / "Installed" line is a
real check that stops with plain words when the tool is not there (`found <cmd> <name>`). Guard
`selfcheck_getstarted.mac_walk` (bitten by putting the redirect back on a copy).

## Never-again: a setup that needs a terminal (2026-10-23 AstroStage MCP, owner: "No one should open the terminal")

Connect your AI shipped as "paste this line into a terminal / config file": the owner ran it in the VPS console, then typed
the question into PowerShell, then asked why a user must do any of it. Rule for every connect / set-up flow on every portal:
the non-technical road comes FIRST and needs only buttons - the user signs in with their existing portal account inside the
other app (OAuth: discovery + dynamic registration + PKCE, an Allow page on our own site), the screen shows a LIVE status the
server sets the moment the other side first calls, and a Check button runs the real round trip and says which step failed in
plain words. Terminal lines, keys and config files live only under a folded "For developers". Built once, generic:
`services/mcp_oauth.py` + `routers/mcp_oauth.py` + `routers/well_known.py`, knobs and words in chip `mcp.server`. Guards
`backend/tests/test_mcp_oauth.py` (8 rules bitten on in-memory copies) and the end-to-end Allow flow run against the live
local servers before hand-over.

## Agents, Daily health check and Connect your AI - core, every portal (2026-10-23, owner: "go with your pick")

The AstroStage agent runtime and MCP door, built ONCE in `tv/core` so every portal on this framework (tv, SteadyMove,
Katha, the SimplyFile family) has them. No per-agent and no per-tool code anywhere - a module only attaches pieces:

| Piece | Core file | A module adds | Its words / rules |
|---|---|---|---|
| Agent recipe | `core/agents.py` | chip `agents.recipe.<id>` (inputs, steps, triggers: schedule or event) | `ui.agents` name_<id> / about_<id> |
| Waiting for you | `core/waiting.py` | `waiting.register(kind, apply=None)`; an agent step returns `_offers` and the generic action `waiting.offer` adds them | `waiting.messages`, card words in `ui.shell` (once per person per key, ever) |
| Agent step | `core/agents.py` | `agents.action("x.y", fn)` fn(args, ctx) - honour `ctx["preview"]` for writes | row in chip `agents.actions` (side effect read/write/external/paid) |
| Event | `core/agents.py` | `agents.emit("job.stopped", facts)` | a recipe trigger `{"kind":"event","event":..,"when":{..}}` |
| Health probe | `core/health.py` | `health.probe("id", fn)` fn() -> {ok, say} | `health.messages` probe_<id>, the module's own words |
| MCP tool | `core/mcp.py` | `mcp.tool("name", fn)` fn(args, ctx) -> dict, READ-ONLY | row in chip `mcp.tools` (schema, Access Control function under `connect-ai`) |

Rules (the AstroStage ones, chips copied unchanged): chips `agents.step_when` / `agents.step_gate` decide run / preview / skip -
a dry run reads for real and only previews writes, Approve runs it for real; a triggered run goes through `agents.trigger_gate`
(all writes ticked `trusted` = runs by itself; else waits for the owner's approval, bell). Schedules: chip
`agents.schedule_due` (UTC; the page shows the viewer's own zone). Health: `health.warn` REPLACES the owners' warning bar
(shell `healthBanner`) and rings once per new problem - never a pile of mails (feedback alert-once). Connect your AI follows
the no-terminal never-again above: sign-in from the AI app (`core/mcp_oauth.py`: discovery, register, Allow card on the
same screen, PKCE tokens) + live status + Check come first; keys and the command only under the folded For developers.
Guards: `tv/selfcheck.py agents_and_mcp_complete` (every recipe step attached + described, every tool row attached, the
probes present, both screens on the shell with a Guide me and a tour, the sign-in road served, keys folded - bite-tested)
and the self-checks of `core.agents`, `core.health`, `core.mcp`, `core.mcp_oauth`, `core.jobs`.

## Never-again: a top bar that runs off a tablet (2026-10-23 AstroStage Studio)

At 820px (iPad Air) the Studio top bar ran 86px off-screen: Utilities gone, the avatar over Portal Admin; at 1024 and 1280 the
labels wrapped to two lines and stood 82px tall in a 64px bar. A desktop nav with eight menus does not fit a tablet in one row,
whatever the gaps. Rule: a nav label never wraps (`whitespace-nowrap`); below lg the nav takes its own row under the brand
(`order-last w-full flex-wrap`, pages that fill the window subtract that row), from lg it sits beside the brand, whose words
wait for xl; text-xs until 2xl. Measure at 820 / 1024 / 1280 / 1536 in the browser (row `scrollWidth` == `clientWidth`, every
item inside the bar) before calling it fixed. Guard `src/topBar.test.ts` (bitten by dropping `whitespace-nowrap` and
`order-last`).

## Never-again: a key to copy between two of our own portals (2026-10-25 Fy Issue > Push to)
The Push to dialog asked the owner to make a CodyFy key and paste it. Every portal already runs an OAuth 2.1 door
(`core/mcp_oauth.py`: register, Allow card, PKCE token, refresh), so a portal is just another app: **Connect <target>** =
`core/push.py connect_start` (register once per return address, PKCE, one-time state kept owner-only in `push_keys`) ->
the target's Allow page -> `/api/core/issues/push/back` (not signed in; the state is the proof) -> tokens kept, refreshed
before they run out (a refused refresh = not connected, Connect offered again). The back page carries NO inline script or
style (script policy): words in `data-*`, `core-static/push-back.js` tells the opener tab over a same-site
`BroadcastChannel` (survives `window.opener` being cut) and drops the code from the address bar. Paste-a-key stays only
as a folded fallback. Guard `python -m core.push` (stand-in OAuth door: Deny, one-use state, refresh, refused refresh).

## Never-again: an API guard that cannot see included routers (2026-10-26 AstroFy)
FastAPI 0.14x keeps `include_router` routers whole (`_IncludedRouter.original_router.routes`) instead of copying their
routes into `app.routes`. AstroFy's guard listed `app.routes` to learn which `/api/` paths were its own, saw none of the
module routes on the VPS (newer FastAPI than the laptop) and let signed-out calls through. Walk included routers
recursively (`app/portal.py api_segments`) and test it with a nested fake (`tests/test_portal.py
test_every_module_api_is_guarded`). After any deploy, curl one module route signed out: it must answer 401.

## Never-again: a visitors read that drops finished visits (2026-10-26 ValueFy)

`core/ledger` writes ONE `views` row per view and the leave beacon puts `dwell_ms` onto that SAME row
(`ON CONFLICT(view_id) DO UPDATE`). A report that reads arrivals with `dwell_ms IS NULL` silently loses every visit
whose reader moved on - ValueFy's Activity showed only the seeded rows. Read every row; the row IS the arrival.
Guard: `D:/ValueFy/selfcheck.py store_flow` (sends the leave beacon, then expects the visit), bite-tested.

A public page at a portal's `/` (ValueFy `static/valuefy.html`, the showcase) sends a `#/screen` link on to `/start`,
where the portal lives - a tour, invite or bell opened at `/#/vfadmin` otherwise stopped on the public page.

A public page served at more than one depth (ValueFy `/` and `/app/<slug>`) links its files from the root
(`/static/...`, `/core-static/...`), never relative: under `/app/<slug>` a relative `static/valuefy.js` asked for
`/app/static/valuefy.js`, got the HTML page back and drew a blank screen. Guard: ValueFy `selfcheck.py api_check`
(no relative src/href on `/app/<slug>`), bite-tested.

A shared button's words come with it: a page that draws core History (`historyButton()` from `static/changes.js`) must
`loadWords('<screen>', 'changes')` - ValueFy's App store loaded only its own words and every row read `[changes.open]`.
Guard: ValueFy `selfcheck.py static_checks` (a page-*.js with historyButton and no 'changes' words fails), bite-tested.

## Never-again: ssh from a portal that hangs after the command ends (2026-10-27 ValueStream CC New Site, vf.valuestream.in)

CC runs from `start.cmd`, so its server owns a console window. `subprocess.run(["ssh", ...])` with no `input=` handed
that console to Windows `ssh.exe` as stdin, and ssh.exe stays open after the remote command finishes. Every step then hit
its timeout (exit 124: "Could not check ports ... timed out after 60s") even though the VPS showed it done. From a shell
without a console the same call returned in 0.6 s, so it looked impossible to reproduce. Rule: every ssh/scp
`subprocess` call passes `stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL` when it sends no input. Reproduce it inside a REAL console
(`Start-Process python repro.py`), not from an agent shell. Guard: the `ssh stdin DEVNULL` block in
`D:\ValueStreamCC\selfcheck.py`.

## Never-again: a video blur that writes nothing (2026-10-27 SteadyMove Videos, Hide personal info)

ffmpeg `boxblur` checks its radius against EACH plane: on yuv420 the colour planes are half size, so the radius must be
at most a quarter of the box's short side (a 64 px name box failed "chroma_param radius value 20, must be <= 16" and the
job wrote no file). `tv/privacy.py blur_filter` caps it at `min(w,h)//4 - 1`; its self-check RUNS the graph through ffmpeg
on a small and a wide box. A personal-info pass always writes a NEW recording (`narrator.new_project`), never over the
original, and the paid picture pass shows its price first (`askCost`, chip `privacy.estimate`).
